714. JOHN JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 1 pair of shoes, value 4s., the goods of John Blake.
JOHN WILLIAM BLAKE. I am the son of George Blake, of Constitution-row, Gray's Inn-road. These shoes hung on some nails in the shop; the prisoner came in last Wednesday, about half-past seven o'clock - my father had known him about three years ago; he asked if my father was at home - I told him he was not: he said I could do as well - he wanted three or four pairs of shoes for a person at Finchley; he gave me the measure: I looked them out, and while I was making out the bill, he sat down in the shop, where the shoes were, within his reach - I made out the bill; he said he was going to the haberdasher's, and would return in about a quarter of an hour; he left - I looked, and missed a pair of women's shoes: I went after him - he was not above twenty or thirty yards off; I asked him where he was going to - he said to the haberdasher's; I said "I think you have got a pair of shoes;" he shook his pockets, and said, "No, I have not, upon my honour"- I felt, and found a pair in his pocket; I took him back, and sent for a constable.
BENJAMIN BARNETT. I have a pair of shoes; the prisoner took them out of his left-hand pocket, and gave them to me - he went down on his knees, and wished me to let him go.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 68.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280221-237]
715. EBENEZER JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of November, 30 lbs. of feathers, value 45s.; 2 pillows, value 4s.; 1 washing tub, value 2s.; 4 irons, value 4s.; 2 curtains, value 5s.; 1 pair of sheets, value 5s.; 2pillow-cases, value 1s.; 1 cushion, value 2s.; 1 tea-kettle, value 1s. 6d.. and 1 pail, value 2s. 6d., the goods of William Shaw.
WILLIAM SHAW. I live in Back-church-lane, Commercial-road. The prisoner took my lodgings about eighteen months ago; he stayed about twelve months; he took them at 4s. 6d. per week; it was the top room furnished; these articles were let with the room: he left, without notice, four or five months back; he owed four week's rent: I afterwards examined the rooms, every thing was gone except the bedstead; I found that the bed had been cut, and the feathers taken out; about thirty pounds of feathers were gone; I have found some of the things since. He has a wife and two children. I saw him, about two months back, down by Hoare's brewhouse, but did not speak to him - I had before given information to the officers he was taken up about a fortnight ago.
Cross-examined by MR. C. PHILLIPS. Q. When did you give information? A. About three months since; if I had spoken to him, I should have been knocked about - his wife did not go by the name of Sophia Mendoza; my son's name is Shaw - his name was Jones; I did not, while lodging with me, know that the woman was not his wife; I had never seen him since but that once.
ROBERT MARSTON. I took the prisoner on the 8th of this month, on Tower-hill; I told him that I apprehended him for the robbery at Mr. Shaw's; he said he did not do it, but that the woman did, and Mrs. Shaw knew it for they had a fight about taking away the things.
Cross-examined. Q. The prisoner had always borne an excellent character? A. I have known him for years; I met him by accident: I had been in search of him, but did not find him till that day. He had got his living by being a City porter, and was so up to the time that I apprehended him - I have been twenty times down to the wharfs to look for him. The woman, Sophia Mendoza, is a bad character.
WILLIAM SHAW re-examined. The prisoner did ask me to get an officer to frighten her out; it was the proposition of some person or persons, but not of mine - I did not frighten her, but the officer did, but not from my proposition. His wife's name was Jones; she went by the name of Mendoza; that was her father's name. I heard a quarrel going on between the prisoner and the woman - I would not have interfered if they had called out murder for a week: he did not tell me she was stealing the feathers - he never told me that the woman was robbing me. I never knew from him, or any other person, that she was stripping the premises, nor did I go up to see in consequence, before he was gone.
JOHN LATTIMER. I have known the prisoner for thirty years; he is a hard working, sober, honest man.
Prisoner's Defence. Lattimer was sent to the prosecutor to know what the woman had made away with; he said that the amount was 7l.; I said I was aware that it was not 10l., and I would rather meet the charge.
JOHN LATTIMER re-examined. As near as I can recollect, I saw the prisoner in Tower-street; he applied to me and said he was in a dilema; he had been living with a creature who had robbed their lodgings, and there was a warrant out against him at the Thames Police; I said"Your are in a pretty scrape;" he said he went home and found the feathers gone, and asked Shaw to come up and see: I went to Shaw, and said to him "You have a man named Jones living with Mendoza, the fighting man's daughter;" he said that the prisoner had had the woman to the Thames Police, and that he ought at that time to have left her, but he was always coming after her - I asked what damage was done, and he said he did not know, but"I will transport him; I can, if it is six months." I told him what the prisoner had stated to me in Tower-street, and that I had come down to tell him; he said she had taken away every thing piecemeal, but he would not apply to her, he would have Jones. I think that he did say that he knew her name to be Mendoza - I said "Jones with the daughter of Mendoza the fighting man;" he said "Yes, the same." I asked what the damage was; he said he could not exactly say what the feathers and irons were worth: I think he said about 7l.; I asked him if he thought Jones had taken anything, he said "He must, they lived together."
WILLIAM PRESS. I heard Shaw say he would compound, if any person would come on behalf of him. I will swear that Shaw said the woman had taken the things, and said something about 7l. It might be three weeks before the prisoner was taken up, that they called on Shaw: I did not know where the prisoner lived; I saw him at work - I went at Mr. Lattimer's request.
JOHN LATTIMER re-examined. I did not like the concern; I did not like to meddle with it, and asked Press to go over too. I thought the longer the prisoner staid away the better; it might be five weeks after. I did not tell Shaw where Jones might be found. Shaw never said that he had taken the woman up, nor that she had stolen the property. Jones went out first; it was about seven o'clock, when he left; the woman went out after. He stated that the woman had been taken to Lambeth Street before, but not for his property.
WILLIAM SHAW re-examined. I had heard that the woman was taken to Lambeth-street Office; an officer came to my house and ran up stairs; he asked if a woman named Jones lived there; I said Yes; he said he wanted her: she returned the same day - she was not committed. Shaw was in the habit of coming home every night to the premises.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-238]
716. LOUISA HESKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of February, 1 shawl, value 20s., the goods of Mary Varrell, spinster.
MARY VARRELL. I live in Norton-street, Portland-road. I lost a shawl out of my box, between six and seven o'clock in the morning of the 6th of February; the prisoner lodged in the same apartment, and was to go away that morning: she slept in the same bed with me. My box was not locked; I am quite sure it was there - she had nothing in the room; she had slept there for a month - her mother lived in Middlesex-buildings. I got up about eight o'clock and went to my box to get some money; I had 10s. 7 1/2d. there; it was gone: the landlady, who slept in the same room, rose at five o'clock. I saw the shawl at the pawnbroker's on the Saturday evening following - I never had lent it to her; I only went there on the previous Saturday.
HENRY ROW. I am shopman to Mr. Morris, pawnbroker, High-street, Mary-le-bone. I took this shawl in of the prisoner - I had seen her before; it was on the 6th of February - I advanced 5s. on it in the name of Maria Wheatley, of William-street, by which name we had done business with her.
HENRY STOWELL. I am an officer of High-street, Mary-le-bone. The prisoner came to the office to swear to the loss of a duplicate; Mary Varrell had told me of the loss of her shawl - the prisoner was not sworn, but I took her into custody.
Prisoner's Defence. I took the shawl on the Wednesday morning, when I left - I took it to buy a pair of shoes to go to my uncle's; and if I had got the shoes, and gone there, I should have had money, and returned it that very evening.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280221-239]
717. DIANA HARDING was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of February, 3 sheets, value 6s; 1 petticoat, value 6d.; 1 handkerchief, value 6d, and 1 shirt, value 1s., the goods of Henry Collard.
SUSANNAH COLLARD. I am the wife of Henry Collard, a shoemaker, of Red Lion-market. The prisoner and I had been drinking together on the evening this matter happened; we had been drinking in my house, and in a public-house - we were all the worse for liquor; I do not know that the prisoner took the property - they were all separated about the room; it was my servant's fault: the sheets were at the fire airing - the shirt was in the drawers- she bid me a good night; I do not know where she lives - I know her well; I think it was about half-past eleven o'clock; she took nothing that I saw - nor do I know who took them; I saw the things the next day at Mr. Barnes's house.
JOSEPH GRAY. I am superintendent of the Houseless Poor in Grub-street. The prisoner came there with two others drunk - they were Jane Moss and Ann Evans; the prisoner had this bundle - they were all intoxicated, but not so bad but they could walk; they came about half-past twelve or one o'clock on the 13th instant; I questioned them, and finding they had a bundle I sent them to the watch-house: Harding said she had bought the things in the bundle in Petticoat-lane - I saw Collard the next morning.
JOHN BARNES. I was officer of the night. The prisoners were brought in, and said that they lodged in Playhouse-yard; I went there the next morning - Mrs. Collard came up and asked for the same three girls; I asked her what she wanted, and she said they had robbed her; the prisoner told me she bought the things in Rosemary-lane - they were wet.
SUSANNAH COLLARD. I went to Playhouse-yard to see if they could give me any intelligence of the things I had lost - the things I saw at the watch-house were mine; I do not know how I lost them - there is no mark on the sheets; I think they are like mine - there were six on the horse; the handkerchief looks like mine - it is the same pattern - the shirt is my little boy's.
Prisoner's Defence. A man stood at Mrs. Collard's door, and asked her to come out; she said, "Come in, girls, and have something to drink" - we went in, she quarrelled with her servant, and turned her out: there was a man there - he went out - Mrs. Collard gave us something to drink; the bundle was at the door, and I took it up, and as it was too late to go home we went to the Refuge.
MRS. COLLARD re-examined. My husband was out - there might be a man there.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280221-240]
718. SAMUEL HENRY HIVALL was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of January, 4 wine glasses, value 1s. 4d, the goods of Barnet Abrahams.
BARNET ABRAHAMS. I live in Great Peter-street, Westminster, and am a broker. On the 16th of January I lost four wine glasses - they were down an area about a foot and a half off; I saw them about half an hour before - they are worth about 16d; I have seen the prisoner about Westminster.
MARIA MORRIS. I am servant to Mr. Abrahams. I was minding the shop - the glasses were on a table down in the area; I did not see the prisoner before he took the glasses from the table - it was not far from the ground; the table was higher than the ground - persons passing could not take them without reaching over; it was between six and seven o'clock in the evening - I think on the 16th of January; the lamps were lighted; he came to the table; I told him to go away, or I would call my master - he used a bad expression, and before I could call my master, the glasses and the prisoner were gone: I saw him afterwards in custody of the officer - there was no other person near the table; I saw the officer before the prisoner was taken, and had described him both to the officer and to my master.
GEORGE JAMES JENNINGS. I am an officer. On the evening of the 16th I learnt that the glasses were taken; a few minutes after they were taken, the prosecutor came to me, and said young Hivall had taken them - that he knew that it was him by the description of the girl; I knew the prisoner before - I took him the next evening; the glasses have not been found.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-241]
719. GEORGE HUNNAM was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of January, 1 half crown, the money of Henry Bull.
HANNAH BULL. I am the wife of Henry Bull, and live in Ratcliffe-highway. The prisoner, at half past ten o'clock on Sunday morning, the 27th of January, came into my shop, and asked for two sixpences for a shilling; I went into the parlour to give him the two sixpences - the parlour is very dark; I brought my bowl of silver out; I had four half crowns among it: I was handing the two sixpences to him, when he grasped my silver and said,"Excuse my freedom, Ma'am, I thought you had a new shilling there, I have had two that was bad;" he had the two sixpences, and went out saying, "I wish you a good morning, Ma'am, I hope you find the shop answer:" I thought that there was some of the silver gone - there was more silver gone than the half crown; my husband came in - I followed the prisoner about a quarter of a mile; Icame up to him near Shadwell church - I said, "You are the man that I gave change to, you have taken a half crown;" he said, "Don't make a bother, Ma'am, here's a half crown; I'd sooner give you one than have a bother."
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Are you sure you had four half crowns in the bowl? A. I am; I told the prisoner he had robbed me - my husband did not say that he had taken one to pay for meat; I collared the prisoner - he did not say that his character was worth more than a half-crown.
GUILTY.
Confined One Year and Publicly Whipped.
[trialAccount t18280221-242]
Fifth Middlesex Jury - Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
720. WILLIAM RIDER was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of December, 2 bushels of mixed corn, called horse corn, value 4s., the goods of Stephen Trehern.
STEPHEN TREHERN. I keep the White Horse public-house, at Uxbridge. On the 31st of December my son caught this man in the act of stealing some mixed corn from my stable at the Red Lion public-house, at Southall, where I keep post horses; here is a sample of it, and I believe it to be mine - here is barley and white pease and Archangel oats, and some other corn mixed together.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Do other persons keep their horses there as well as you? A. Yes, there are a great many horses there; I do not know what they eat.
WILLIAM TREHERN. I am the prosecutor's son. The prisoner goes about with a cart - I saw him at my father's stable door at Southall, on the 31st of December - he had this corn in a sack under some hay in his cart; I went up to him, and asked what business he had there - he said, he only came for a little rubbishing hay to make hands; we rent the stable of the person who keeps the Red Lion public-house: I made the prisoner turn out the hay, and when I saw the corn, I said, "What is here?" - he said,"I don't know;" I opened and found it was our mixtures, which we send up weekly - I compared it, and it matched exactly - it is an unusual mixture; I was on horseback: I do not know how long he had been in the cart - I missed some corn from my father's loft, where there were about forty bushels, which had been shot on the preceding day.
Cross-examined. Q. But might not that have gone to feed your horses? A. It might - I cannot say how long his cart might have been there - there was some loose hay in it: I have not brought any of my father's mixture here, only a sample of what was found in the cart.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-243]
721. JAMES PHILPOT AUSTIN was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of January, 4 yards of printed cotton, value 8s., and 1 rule, value 7s., the goods of Samuel Pratt, the elder, and Samuel Pratt, the younger, his masters.
The prisoner pleaded GUILTY. Aged 22.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Four Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-244]
722. MARGARET VAUGHAN was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of January, 1 shawl, value 5s., the goods of Mary White, Spinster.
MARY WHITE. I lodge in Mary-le-bone-court, up three pair of stairs. On the 24th of January the prisoner came to my door and knocked - I said, "Come in;" she said, she wanted to speak to me: she asked if I knew a person of the name of Olcrenshaw - I said Yes, and she said she wanted the loan of my yellow shawl; I said, she had shawls enough - she said she had been out the night before, and had lost her shawl; and she wanted to speak to me and could not come out without one: I lent it to her, and have not seen her since.
MARY ANN OLCRENSHAW. I never sent the prisoner for a shawl; I knew her for some time - she had lived where I had used to call to see a friend.
Prisoner's Defence. I met her the night before, and asked her to lend me a shawl - she said she had not one, but if I went to the prosecutrix I might borrow one of her: I had the misfortune to lose it.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280221-245]
723. JOHN FOX was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 6 shillings and 3 sixpences, the monies of John Jones.
ELIZABETH JONES. I am the wife of John Jones - we live in Maiden-lane. On the 4th of February I went into the shop to snuff the candle, and found the prisoner under the counter; he rose up, and I caught hold of his collar: I found the till drawn half way out, and the bowl in which the silver had been in the till, laying by his side on the ground; I had seen it safe when he came in at half-past seven - he asked for a pint of small beer - I said I would rather not serve him; he asked again, and then I said I would draw it for him if he would go out again directly - he put me down a sixpence, and then I saw the money was all safe in my till - there was six shillings and three sixpences; he then had a pennyworth of bread, and four pennyworth of meat, which came to the whole of his sixpence - he was at this time sitting on a form in the shop.
JAMES GROOM. Mrs. Jones's girl came to my house, and asked me to step in - I went in and found the prisoner there; Mrs. Jones had him by the collar - she said, "He has taken out my till, and got out my money" - he said,"I have no money but four shillings of my own about me."
BENJAMIN PARRETT. I took the prisoner - he produced 7s. 6d. and one halfpenny, which he said was his own money.
JOHN HUTT. I am the watch-house keeper - I produce these six shillings and three sixpences, which I received from Parrett.
Prisoner's Defence. I went to the shop - I asked for a pint of beer, which I got; I then asked if she had any cooked meat - she said, "No, but here are some saveloys;" I said, "Just warm one," which she did - I then had a penny loaf, and gave some persons who were in the shop part of the beer: I then knocked at the room door - Mrs. Jones came out and said, "What do you want here?" - I said I wanted another pint of beer, and that I had called for it twice, and that she had not heard me; she then asked the two persons in the shop if I had - they said they were asleep, and had not heard me: she then went to the counter, and said, "My till is out, and I have lost four or five shillings I know" - I said,
"I have six shillings and three sixpences of my own money."
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.

724. NATHANIEL DAVIS was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of January, 1 watch, value 2l.; 1 watch-chain, value 10s., and 1 seal, value 6s., the goods of John Ward.
The prisoner pleaded GUILTY. Aged 31.

[trialAccount t18280221-247]
725. WILLIAM CLARK, EDWARD WAGHORN, and THOMAS FOWLER were indicted for stealing, on the 26th of January, 1 book, value 3s., the goods of Thomas Batemen.
THOMAS BATEMAN. I am a bookseller, and live in Goodge-street, Tottenham-court-road. I lost a book on the 26th of January, but did not miss it till the officers told me of it; it had been inside my window, among some others, for several weeks; I am not certain when I had seen it, but I know it by the label.
WILLIAM JORDAN. I am an officer. I saw the three prisoners together, in Leather-lane, that Saturday afternoon, about two o'clock; I watched them through several streets, to Gray's Inn-lane, where I met Cadby and Colton - we all followed them for five or six miles, through Camden-town and other places; when they got to Goodge-street, I saw Fowler take this book - the other two were close at his elbow; I told Colton and Cadby, who immediately ran across, and before I crossed they had got the three prisoners into a shop.
JOSEPH CADBY. I was with Jordan, and followed the prisoners through a number of streets; I saw Fowler take the book, and the other two were close to him; I ran over and took Clark and Fowler into a linen-draper's shop.
WILLIAM COLTON. I was following the prisoners - I saw Fowler take the book, and give it to Waghorn, who ran off with it, at the end of Goodge-street - I pursued and took him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
CLARK'S Defence. I was looking for a situation - I saw Fowler, and asked if he knew whether it had gone five o'clock; before he could speak, the officers came, and pushed us into a shop.
CLARK - GUILTY. Aged 20.
WAGHORN - GUILTY. Aged 21.
FOWLER - GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-248]
726. JOHN ABRAHAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of February, 9 seal-skins, value 27s., the goods of Solomon Phillips.
SOLOMON PHILLIPS. I am a seal-skin manufacturer, and live in Fishmonger-alley, Borough, my factory is in Redcross-yard, Borough. On Friday, the 1st of February, I counted my seal-skins, and had seventy - I went home, and did not return till the Sunday morning - I then missed nine skins, but I had before heard that some were missing; I have seen three of them since, which I know to be mine, from the marks on them - each man puts his own particular mark upon his skins.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Was not the prisoner your servant? A. Yes, and up to this time he had a good character - I will take him again.
MICHAEL HUGHES. I am a watchman. On the morning of the 2d of February, I was in Paternoster-row, Spitalfields, and saw the prisoner with a bundle under his arm; I asked what he had got; he said his work, which he had brought from the Borough, and that he was going to take it to a house in Lamb-street, next door to a dry-salters; I followed him, and as he did not seem to go into any house, I asked him again, where he was going; he said, "Stop, I will tell you - the skins are my father's;" I took hold of them, and he ran away - I pursued, and took him.
THOMAS HART. I was beadle of the night. On the morning of the 2d of February, the prisoner was brought into the watch-house with these skins, which he said were his father's.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-249]
727. THOMAS FITT was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 12 ozs. weight of tobacco, value 3s., the goods of William Henry Isaac.
WILLIAM HENRY ISAAC. I am a tobacconist, and live in Upper York-street. On the 17th of January, between six and seven o'clock in the evening, I received information from some little girls, and pursued the prisoner; I saw him opposite the Yorkshire Stingo public-house, with two or three other persons; I seized him, put my hand to his pocket, and felt this parcel of tobacco; he begged I would not give him in charge; but wished me to go with him to Stingo-lane, and he would hand me over another parcel of tobacco, which he had given to another boy; I refused, but took him into a shop; he gave me the name of the other boy, which was Collier - he wished to go away, and offered to leave his coat; I left him in the shop, and went with my brother to Stingo-lane, but could find no such person; when I returned, he told the officers the name of Collier, and they took him; he said the prisoner had thrown away one packet of tobacco; when I first took him he said he took it from my window, and begged I would not give him in charge - they are packets of Dutch C'naster, and I have brought another to shew the similarity; I had three packets of this sort, and all in blue papers; I have not opened them, but they feel like tobacco - I had them from a very respectable house; I have not any dummies in my shop.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Had you any mark of your own on this parcel, before it was taken? A. No; I did not say I would not hurt him, if he would tell me where any of the other boys lived; nor anything of the kind; I will not swear that this parcel was not mixed with others before I marked it - I marked it in my shop, before it went to the office - it was then laying on my shop-counter - I can only swear I had packets similar to this, and lost packets similar to this.
RICHARD HARTLEY WALL. I was not the officer who was sent for, but I received this tobacco from Buckbridge, who is now dangerously ill.
Cross-examined. Q. Before you got it, where had it been kept? A. In the cupboard at the office.
GUILTY - Aged 16.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280221-250]
728. JAMES CAMP was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of February, 1 pair of breeches, value 10s., the goods of James West.
JAMES WEST. I live at Enfield, and am servant to Mr. Cracknold. I had a box of clothes in a shed, in his orchard - I kept the key of it; I put the things into it about noon, on the Thursday, while I was working in the garden. On Saturday, the 9th of February, the prisoner, whom I knew, came to me, and asked if I had lost anything; I said No; he said, "I think you have, look in your box, and see if you have lost any clothes;" I went to look, and the box was gone: he then said he had found the box open, in a ditch, as he was coming to work; there was a pair of black breeches and a waistcoat in it; I said, "There was a pair of corded breeches in it, have you seen them?" he said No, he had not; I have since seen a pair of corded breeches, which I believe are mine - I had bought them the week before of Mrs. Parker, and had only worn them on the Sunday.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Is Mrs. Parker here? A. Her man is; I found these breeches at Mr. King's.
JAMES STRINGER. I am shopman to Mrs. Parker; I sold a pair of breeches to the prosecutor - these are like them; I do not see any difference, but I cannot swear to them.
Cross-examined. Q. You have a great many more of the same sort? A. Yes; I never sold any to the prisoner but Mrs. Parker might.
THOMAS SIMKINS. The prosecutor lodges with me; I saw him have a pair of breeches on like these, which he said he bought of Mrs. Parker, and he should not wear them again till Easter.
JAMES KING. I am a pawnbroker. On the 11th of February these breeches were pawned with me - I cannot say by whom.
JOHN MEAD. I am a constable of Enfield. I was before the Magistrate when the prisoner was examined; what he said was taken down - he had it read to him, and made his mark; this is Magistrate's signature - (read.)
The said J. Camp, the prisoner, being called on by me for what he has to say, and cautioned by me not to say any thing to his own injury, saith, "I did take the box with the clothes out of the tool house; what the first witness has said is true, I did pawn the breeches with Mr. King for 5s., and threw the duplicate in the fields: nobody induced me to tell this; I wished to make it up with the prosecutor."
Prisoner. Mr. Wilson, the constable, told me to say so.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18280221-251]
729. ELIZABETH COATES was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 1 bed, value 50s.; 1 bolster, value 5s.; 2 pillows, value 8s.; 3 blankets, value 10s; 1 pair of sheets; value 4s.; 1 tea-kettle value 3s., and 2 tables, value 5s. the goods of Ann Simons.
ANN SIMONS. The prisoner lodged with me. On the 4th of February she took out my table - she left her children in the room, and when she came back she was taken; I know the whole of these things to be mine; they are part of the furniture which I had let her with the room.
HENRY HAINES. I bought two tables of the prisoner; one on Saturday evening, the 2d of February, at seven o'clock; and the other a little after eight o'clock, on the 4th.
Cross-examined by MR. J. ALLEY. Q. What did you give for the two tables? A. Four and sixpence, which is above the value; but I took compassion on her.
ANN LOOSEMORE. I bought the duplicate of a bed of the prisoner; she said it was a new tick, and a very good one. I sent for the bed; she called upon me again, to know if I could let her have any more, and I said, "Is that a new tick?" she looked round, and said, "Dear me, how came it so dirty?" She then said her aunt had pawned it; and I told her, if she would return the 2s. which I had given her, she might have it again; I afterwards sold it.
JOHN NORRIS. I was sent for to Mrs. Simmons; I found the prisoner crying - she told me where she had sold the tables - I went and got them - she said she had sold the duplicates of the other things - she is married and I believe has three children.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18280221-252]
730. THOMAS CALEY was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of December, 1 carpenter's rule, value 2s., the goods of Henry Wright.
HENRY WRIGHT. I am a carpenter. I lent this rule on the 17th of December - I know it to be mine.
ABRAHAM STANNARD. Wright lent me this rule on the 17th of December, and I was using it on the latter part of that day, at a house in Brunswick-street, Hackney-road; Wright came next morning to ask me for it, and it was gone - the prisoner was at work at the same building, but I had not lent to him.
WILLIAM BRIGG. I live with Mr. Attenborough, pawnbroker. I took in this rule on the 17th of December, in the name of Henry Ward; but I cannot say of whom - this is the duplicate which I gave for it.
JAMES ORFORD. I am an officer. I apprehended the prisoner on the 28th of January - I searched him and found two duplicates in his fob of two rules - one was for this rule which Wright has sworn to.
Prisoner's Defence. I bought the rule of my partner, a mason named Poole; he ran away from the work the Monday before Christmas-day - I continued to work there till I was taken.
ABRAHAM STANNARD re-examined. Q. Did you know a person of the name of Poole? A. Yes; he did work there; he received some money from our master, on the Monday before Christmas-day, to go and pay for some work; he never paid for it; but went away.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-253]
731. ELIZABETH BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of February, 1 tea-kettle, value 2s., the goods of John Mould.
JOHN MOULD. I keep the Green Man and Still public-house, in Coppice-row, Clerkenwell . The prisoner came there on the 14th of February, and asked for a pennyworth of porter; said she was extremely cold, and went into the tap-room, where she remained some time; there were five persons there drinking; she had a black apron on, which she threw over the tea-kettle, and took it away; I received information, pursued, and took her in Ray-street, about fifty yards off with it.
CHARLES CAMERON. I am an officer. I was sent for, and took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 48.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-254]
732. THOMAS BARRETT was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 69lbs. weight of lead, value 13s., and 1 sack, value 2s., the goods of Frederick Charles Burton.
THOMAS HENRY HURT. I work for Mr. Burton. I left his shop in Eagle-street about two o'clock on the 17th of January - he is a plumber and glazier; before I had left I had stripped off some lead - I believe this to be part of it.
Prisoner. Q. Do you know the Cheshire Cheese public-house, in Dean-street? A. Yes - I use it every day for my breakfast and dinner.
JAMES MEDLYCOT. I am an officer. I stopped the prisoner on the 17th of January, in Eagle-street, about half past six o'clock in the evening, with this lead in a sack; I asked what he had got - he said some lead, which he had brought from Penton-place, where he had been working for himself - that it was lead which he had left, and he was going to take it back to Mr. Ransom, in Bedfordbury, of whom he had bought it; I detained him, and went there; they said they knew him, but had not sold him any lead.
DANIEL RIERDON. I am an officer. I was with Medlycot, and staid with the prisoner while he went to Ransom's; while he was gone the prisoner said to me, "I am very sorry I told him wrong - I have had the lead some time, and was going to sell it to get myself a pair of shoes."
FREDERICK CHARLES BURTON. I am a lead-merchant. I had a quantity of lead on my premises; I have compared this with what remains, and I believe it to be the same.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that a man named Davenport had given him the lead to dispose of, at the Cheshire Cheese public-house, for which he promised to give him a pair of new shoes.
THOMAS RANSOM. I am the brother of Mr. Ransom, of Bedfordbury. My brother has employed the prisoner for years, and he always had a good character.
FREDERICK CHARLES BURTON. This witness called at my house, and told my brother that if I would go to a public-house in Broad-street, previous to the hearing, he could give me information of several men who had robbed me - I went there, and saw him; he then said if I would give him a sovereign he would tell me something; I said I would not - he then said he should not tell me anything.
THOMAS RANSOM. I did tell him I could give him some information; but it was only what I derived from the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 31.
Confined Two Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-255]
733. THOMAS HOPKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of January, 1 sovereign, 2 half-crowns, 1 shilling, and 2 sixpences, the goods of Peter Wade, from his person.
PETER WADE. On the 20th of January I was in Whitechapel, about two o'clock in the morning, and had taken a drop too much; I was taken to the watch-house - when I got there I had one sovereign, three half-crowns, two shillings, and two sixpences; I had taken it for my wages about a quarter before twelve o'clock that night - I then went and drank, but did not pay for any thing - my master paid for what I had; I was put into a room where the prisoner and some other persons were; I lost my money in the night, and the next morning I saw some money taken from the prisoner's shoes; I was three parts drunk, and do not know what I had done before I went into the watch-house.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Is your master here? A. No. I was taken near the church in Whitechapel - I believe they took me to the watch-house for safety; I had a long way to go home: I live at Stepney- my master is Mr. Cann, a hair-dresser, living in Fleet-street; I received my money at his shop, and then went with him to a public-house in Fetter-lane; I and three more drank nine or ten glasses of gin and water. In going from the public-house to Whitechapel a number of men and women spoke to me; several females came very near to me: after I left the public-house I showed my money to two of my shopmates, named Scott and Sinclair.
THOMAS SMITH. I am beadle of Whitechapel. On Sunday morning, the 20th of January, the prisoner was brought to the watch-house, but I am not certain whether he was searched or not; while he was there I brought in the prosecutor from High-street, the worse for liquor; I do not know what he had about him, but about six o'clock the next morning he said he was very sorry, and hoped I would let him go home; I said, "Have you got every thing safe about you?" he then felt, and said he had lost a sovereign, some half-crowns, and some silver; I went into the room where the others were, and brought out the prisoner; I found a sovereign, two shillings, and two sixpences in his left waistcoat pocket, and one half-crown in each of his shoes - he said he had it from his father. I saw an apron taken out of a hat, but I do not know whose it was.
Cross-examined. Q. What did the prosecutor say he was sorry for having done? A. I was dispersing some low men and women, and he came in among them, and asked what I wanted to do; he was a good deal the worse for liquor - he had a watch when he went in, but I did not see any money.
WILLIAM HEATH. I am a patrol of Whitechapel. I assisted in taking the prisoner to the watch-house, for rescuing a girl from a watchman, in White Lion-street; I saw him searched the next morning, and the money found - Dungate took his hat off, and found in it this apron, which was claimed by Wade; I heard the prosecutor say he had lost 1l. 10s. 6d. - I think he said a sovereign, four half-crowns, and a sixpence; I did not search the other persons who were there, as they were only put in for being disorderly, fighting, and such like, which is frequently the case in that part of the town.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you search the prisoner when he went into the watch-house? A. No - I searched the other persons who went in; and one of them had no money when he went in, but the next morning there was half a crown and a shilling found upon him.
JOHN DUNGATE. I am an officer. I took the apron out of the prisoner's hat, and found a half-crown in each of his shoes - I did not search him when he went it.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see the prosecutor brought in? A. Yes; he certainly was very drunk - I was in an adjoining room; I was not in the hole with the prisoner. They have no candle nor fire.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-256]
SIXTH DAY. WEDNESDAY, FEBRUARY 27.
Fifth Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Common Sergeant.
734. BRIDGET RYAN and ELIZABETH MARLOW were indicted for stealing, on the 21st of January, 1 watch, value 20s.; 1 watch-key, value 20s.; 1 watchchain, value 1s.; and 1 watch guard, value 1s., the goods of John King, from his person.
JOHN KING. On the 21st of January, as I was going home, at two o'clock in the morning, I saw two men ill using the prisoner Ryan; she asked me for protection; I went with her to her own room; I went to bed, and put my watch under the pillow, and in the morning, when I was putting my stockings on, I saw her take my watch and boots and go out, I said "Give me my watch;" she said I should have it; I could not follow, as I was undressed. Marlow had burst the door open, and asked Ryan if she wanted any gin; she fetched the gin, and, in half an hour, she took the things out of the room. I took Ryan at her own room next morning; she said she was very sorry she had committed the robbery - I am sure she said "robbery." I told the Magistrate so.
JOSEPH HALL. On the 21st of January, Marlow pawned this watch with me, about nine o'clock in the morning; she said she brought it from a friend who was at her house - the prosecutor came in that morning very tipsy.
RYAN'S Defence. I was letting a friend out; the prosecutor came and spoke to me; he went up; he said he had been with a woman before, and she had robbed him; and in the morning, he said he would thank me to go and get eight shillings on his watch, and then he got me to pawn his ring.
JOHN KING. I did not; I had plenty of silver.
MARLOW'S Defence. I saw him give her the watch to pawn, and she gave it to me to get ten shillings on it.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-257]
735. GEORGE DIXON was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of February, 2 cwt. of lead, value 30s., the goods of Thomas Skelton, and fixed to a building of his.
THOMAS SKELTON. I lost this lead from a house, which I let, in Church-lane, St. George's; I know it was all safe on the roof on the 1st of February.
THOMAS SCOTT. I am tenant of this house; the lead was all safe on the 1st of February, as I was on the roof: on the 2d of February it was all cut ready to be taken away - a neighbour came, about seven o'clock that evening, and said there was a man on the top of my house; I went up and found a parcel of lead, loose, on the top of the dormer; it was not rolled up, but cut off, and in about half an hour I again heard there was somebody on the house - I went up with my son, and found another piece cut off, and saw it matched, and it fitted exactly.
WILLIAM HAY. I live at No. 64, Church-lane, two doors from Scott. On the 2d of February, I came from Hackney, and heard some lead was gone: Scott said he thought there was somebody on the house then; I went up and saw the prisoner on the roof, I followed him over the tops of the houses; he got in at a window, and was taken on the first floor of No. 5, Church-lane - it was then near eight o'clock.
OWEN SULLIVAN. I am a watchman. I was at Scott's house, and heard an alarm; I went and took the prisoner, on the first floor of No. 5; I heard something drop from the him, which proved to be this knife.
JOHN JAMES. I am a sailor, and lodge in Rosemary-lane, with the prisoner. On Saturday evening, the 2d of February, he left me about 6 o'clock - I know the knife produced to be his; I have often borrowed it.
WILLIAM SUMMERS. I am an officer. The prisoner was given into my charge; I found nothing upon him - I have fitted the lead; it corresponded exactly with the dormer.
Prisoner's Defence. I had no money to pay for a lodging and went there to sleep.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.

736. SAMUEL PHILLIPS, alias ABRAHAM ABRAHAMS , and CHARLES alias COLLEY BENJAMIN were indicted for stealing, on the 26th of July, 2 sovereigns and one 5l. Bank note, the property of Edmund Watling, their master; against the Statute.
The prisoners having been convicted at Clerkenwell of obtaining the property in question by conspiracy, the case was not proceeded with.
NOT GUILTY.

623. CHARLES ROSSER was indicted for feloniously receiving 2 bars of iron, value 5s. 6d., the goods of our Lord the King , knowing the same to have been stolen.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
GEORGE WALKER. I am a carpenter employed by the Surveyor of Hyde-park. Part of the iron railing was taken away; I found some of it at Mr. Fox's, of Knightsbridge, about one o'clock on the 23d of January.
Cross-examined by MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. Is there anything particular about that railing? A, I know it by putting it up.
JAMES SHEDDEN. I am surveyor of Hyde-park. I went to Fox's house, on the 24th of January, and found these two iron bars, which were taken from the outlet at Hyde-park; and in consequence of what passed, I went to the prisoner's house, in North-street, Knightsbridge - he was out; I sent an officer, who brought him in about five minutes; I asked him how he became possessed of the iron, which was at Fox's; he hesitated, and said he bought them honestly; I asked where, he said of two boys; I said, "Did you know them;" he hesitated, and then said "I don't know;" his wife said "Yes, Rosser, you do know; you had them from young Griffiths;" he said "Yes it was him and another person he did not know - I asked where Griffith's lived; he made no answer, but his wife said, "Just by, in Cross-street, over a stable:" we went in search, but could not find him; I asked the prisoner what he gave for it; he said 1d. for 3lbs., which would be 1s. 10 1/2d. - they are worth 17s. 4d.
Cross-examined. Q. As old iron, would it be worth more than he gave? A. It was not old iron; 17s. 4d. is the new price; he went with me to look for Griffiths - the prisoner keeps an old iron shop.
SAMUEL FOX. I bought these two bars of iron of the prisoner the day before they were found - they are wrought iron.
Cross-examined. Q. As old iron, is one more valuablethan another? A. Yes: I bought them as old iron - they stood openly in his yard.
COURT. Q. Did you think them old iron? A. I bought them as such; I asked him no questions; I had known him living there ten years, and bearing a good character for anything I knew.
THOMAS AMSDEN. I am a patrol of Bow-street. I found the bars in the shop.
GEORGE WALKER. I have no particular mark on them; they are cut off very rough, and are the same size and dimensious; I had fixed them up, and two were pulled down.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-260]

[trialAccount t18280221-261]
739. EDWARD WELCH was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of December, 1 piece of wood, called quartering, value 2s., the goods of our Lord the King .
SECOND COUNT, of the Hon. William Sturges Bourne, William Dacres Adams, Esq., and Henry Dawkins, Esq.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
PETER COOKE BARRAND. I am a carpenter, and live at the Castle, in Moor's-yard. About 7 o'clock in the morning, about two months ago, there was some paling there; I saw the prisoner there; he pulled a piece of quartering out of the ground, and walked away with it on his shoulder; I followed him into Vere-street, and then said, "That is the way the wood goes from Moor's-yard:" he turned round and said, "I may as well take it away as another, my master put it up." I said he did not authorize you to take it away - I saw him take it into a house in Vine-street. I then gave information.
WILLIAM OXLEY. I am a constable. I was sent for, and took the prisoner at a public-house - I got this piece of timber from Andrews.
WILLIAM ANDREWS. I bought this wood of the prisoner; I am in the employ of Mr. M'Pherson, a publican, in Chandos-street - I did not ask where he got it; I bought it for firewood: part has been burned.
JAMES GILBERT. This wood is the property of the Commissioners of Woods and Forests; it was in Moor's-yard under my care.
The prisoner put in a written defence, stating that there were three other persons of his name residing in the street, and that he had not committed the robbery.
GUILTY. Aged 41.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-262]
740. JOHN PHILPOT was indicted for embezzlement.
WILLIAM SHEARMAN. I am a carpenter, and live in Little Anchor-street, Church-street, Bethnal-green; the prisoner worked for me occasionally for about three years; he was in my employ on the 14th of July, 1824; I occasionally employed him to collect money; I told him on that day to receive 1l. 16s. 6d. from Tucker - he should have brought me the money the same morning; I never saw him afterwards till he was taken, and have never received the money.
JOSEPH VERISON TUCKER, In July, 1824, I owed Shearman 1l. 16s. 6d., which I paid to a man - I cannot say whether the prisoner is the man: here is the receipt the person gave me.
WILLIAM SHEARMAN. When I took the prisoner he said, voluntarily, that he had spent Tucker's money, and I ought to have had it.
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-263]
741. SAMUEL WARD was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of November, 1 bushel of mixed corn, called horse-corn, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Deborah Dixon, widow.
MR. COOPER conducted the prosecution.
THOMAS GREENFIELD. I am coachman to Mrs. Deborah Dixon, widow; I drove the Henley coach for her from there to London. On the 3d of November, I stopped with the coach at some private stables at Hounslow, which she rents of Stuckbury; before I changed horses I saw the prisoner at the back of the stable; I got off my coach, and went round to the back stable - I had seen the prisoner there before, and desired him not to remain there - it is a private yard: we have two rest-horses and a corn-bin in the back stable; I saw the prisoner go to the bin, and fill a bag, which he hid under the dunghill - he did not see me; I went up, and said, "Mr. Ward, what have you got there?" he said, "D - n it, here is a great rat;" he took up a brick, and ran round the stable; I went after him, but he crossed the field, and ran away; I went to the dung-heap, and found this corn in the bag - it is called horse-corn; all the corn there belongs to Mrs. Dixon - here is nearly a bushel of it.
Prisoner's Defence. I was never in the stable; I found it under the granary, and threw it on the ground.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-264]
742. JEREMIAH DONOVAN was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of January, 1 cloak, value 30s., the goods of Thomas Larkman.
THOMAS LARKMAN. I keep the Wheatsheaf public-house, Holywell-street, Strand . On the 18th of January this cloak hung in my club-room; I went up about seven o'clock, and saw the prisoner and two young men - the young men went away soon after, but the prisoner seemed loath to go; I said he must go; he went out, but soon returned, and staid in the tap-room; I went out, and returned in ten minutes - he was then gone; I went up stairs, and missed my cloak; I afterwards apprehended him; Berkley gave me the duplicate.
MARIA BERKLEY. I saw the prisoner a few days after this; he gave me the duplicate of a cloak - I gave it up.
JAMES TAGUS SHOOT. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Fetter-lane. On the 19th of January this cloak was pawned by a young woman, not Berkley, in the name of Jesse Moriencourt.
HENRY MORGAN. I am an officer. I took Moriencourt, and she took me to the pawnbroker's where I found the cloak - she was bound over, but is not here; I then took Berkley.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was at his house, and went out to look for Mr. Grantham, and then went home; I afterwards heard of the robbery, and asked Larkman if he suspected me, why he did not give me in charge.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-265]
743. JOHN ROACH was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of January, 1 pair of scales, value 20s.; 1 till, value 2s., and 10 shillings, the property of John Manning.
HARRIET MANNING. I am the wife of John Manning, a baker, of New-street, Covent-garden. On the 16th of January I was at home, and saw the prisoner lurking about the window, from eight to nine o'clock; and about eleven o'clock at night I saw a boy leaving the shop, and believe it to be the prisoner; I then missed a pair of scales off my counter; and my till, containing about 10s.; I was in the parlour - there was nobody in the shop; I gave an alarm, and he was pursued; I saw the prisoner in custody next day.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You only saw the back of the boy who left your shop? A. No, not then.
COURT. Q. You had seen the face of the boy when he was lurking about? A. Yes; that was the prisoner, and, I believe he is the boy who ran out.
ANN MITCHELL. I am servant to Mr. Manning. On the 16th of January, just before eleven o'clock at night, the prisoner came into the shop, and asked me two or three times to let him have three penny biscuits for 1d.; I would not; he went out; I saw him lurking about the window; I went into the parlour, and soon after the scales were gone; I ran out, but could not see him - I believe him to be the boy; the scales are not found - the till was safe when I was in the shop, which was three minutes before.
Cross-examined. Q. Will you swear you saw him in the shop after he came for the biscuits? A. No.
JOHN GIMBER. I am a writer. I was passing the prosecutor's house, about eleven o'clock, and a person passed me, with a box in his hand - he appeared much like the prisoner; I could not see his face; I described him to the officer - I believe him to be the boy - his dress was the same when he was taken.
JOHN GROOM. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, between two and three o'clock on the 17th, from Mrs. Manning's description.
GUILTY. Aged 15.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-266]
744. GEORGE SALES was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of January, 2 1/2lbs. of mutton, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Joshua Walden.
JOHN WILLIAMS. I am a grocer, and live in Golden-lane. On the 12th of January, about twelve o'clock at night, I saw the prisoner by Walden's house, which is next door to me - he went up to the door, took the mutton off the stand, and threw it away a few yards off; I ran and secured him five yards off, on the other side of the way.
JOSEPH WALDEN. I am a butcher, and live two doors from Williams. I was at my door; the prisoner came, and took a neck of mutton - it was thrown into the road; a friend took it up.
ALEXANDER MOORE. The prisoner was given into my charge.
Prisoner's Defence. I was rather intoxicated; I took the meat, and threw it down, not with any felonious intention; I offered to pay for it when they took me - I did not move from the place.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-267]
745. JOHN MURPHY was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of January, 1 half-sovereign, the money of John Cohen, his master.
JOHN COHEN. I am a musician, and live in Leman-street, Goodman's-fields; the prisoner was about four weeks in my employ. On the 20th of January I missed a sovereign, and on the 25th some money was marked; the prisoner behaved very well before this - the half-sovereign was taken on the 26th - he behaved very honest before.
JOHN BRENDLING. I am landlord of the house where Cohen lives. I marked eleven sovereigns and a half-sovereign for him; he put them into a bag, and gave them to his wife; the prisoner was charged with taking a half-sovereign, and gave it up to me; here it is - it is the one I marked.
MARY COHEN. On Saturday Brendling marked some sovereigns, in a bag, which I put into a box, in my bed-room, and locked it; on Sunday morning my husband told me half a sovereign was missing.
JAMES LEE. I apprehended the prisoner on the 26th; I made him no promise; I said, "Where is the half-sovereign?" he said he had taken it out of the box, and given it to Brendling before I got there.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18280221-268]
746. JOHN BOYLE was indicted for stealing, on the 20th February, 3 rasps, value 6s.; 9 copper rivets, value 2d.; 2 hammers, value 1s. 6d.; 1 screw, value 1d.; 1 rivet set, value 1d., and 1 button set, value 1d., the goods of John Braithwaite, his master.
CHARLES LEE. I am shopman to Mr. Braithwaite, a brazier, of New-road, Surrey-square. The prisoner was eight or nine years in his employ - I went to his lodging, in Little Brook-street; I sent for an officer, who found these tools there - they are what we work with; the men never take the tools home, without leave - if he had asked leave he might have taken them; some men work at home, but the prisoner did not - he has only a small room.
WILLIAM HEATH. I am an officer. I found the tools at the prisoner's lodging; he said part of them were his own, and part he had borrowed.
JOHN BRAITHWAITE. The prisoner worked for me - here are several new files, which have never been used - men at times do jobs for themselves.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been in business with another man, but it did not answer, and we divided our tools- I sold them as I could, and lent some; they are common tools. NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-269]
747. JOHN WALKER was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 1 bed, value 5s. 6d.; 1 pillow, value 5s.; 2 blankets, value 5s., and 1 counterpane, value 5s., the goods of Joseph Pynn.
JOSEPH PYNN. I am a ship-carpenter - the prisoner is a labourer in the coal trade. On the 20th of February I delivered these articles to him, in Shakspeare's-walk, Shadwell, to take on board the John and Mary, on the other side the river, and deliver them to Blenkinsey, the mate; I have not seen them since.
ROBERT BLENKINSEY. I was on board the John and Mary, on the 20th of February. I have known the prisoner two or three years; he never brought these articles to me.
JAMES WAYLING. I am an officer; took the prisoner last Friday, and he said it was all settled, but afterwards said he sold the things in Rosemary-lane, which he should not have done if he had not been in liquor.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined Two Months.

748. CHARLES TAYLOR was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of February, 1 piece of mahogany, value 5s.; 1 cap, value 2s.; 1 plane, value 2s.; 1 oil-stone, value 1s.; 1 chisel, value 6d.; 1 guage, value 6d.; 1 hammer, value 1s., and 1 pair of saw-handles, value 1s., the goods of Richard Hewett.
RICHARD HEWETT. I am a cabinet-maker, and live in King-street, Hammersmith. The prisoner worked for me; I lost these tools at different times.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Does he not work at home? A. Yes, I have borrowed tools of him; they are at my place now - some of them have been lost - the wood was found on him, in the shop.
DAVID DAVIE. I am an officer. I went with Hewett to the prisoner's lodging, and found these tools.(Property produced and sworn to.)
NOT GUILTY.

749. JAMES SHEA was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 1 coat, value 5s.; 1 waistcoat, value 2s. 1 pair of trousers, value 5s.; 1 shirt, value 1s., and 1 handkerchief, value 2d., the goods of John Bell.
JANE BELL. I am the daughter of John Bell, shipwright, of Hermitage-street, Wapping. On the 16th of February, about three o'clock in the afternoon, when my father was out, the prisoner came and asked if Mr. Bell lived there; I said Yes; he said he came from the ship for these articles; my father was working on board the Atlas; he said he came from Mr. Bell for them, as he was going out with the captain; I delivered these clothes to him, believing that he came from my father.
JOHN BELL. I know nothing of the prisoner, and never sent him for any thing; I have not found my clothes.
JAMES JEFFREY. I called at the prisoner's house - his brother came and gave himself up; I sent for the witness, who said he was not the man; I said if the prisoner did not come I should come and take him; he came, and she said he was the man.
The Prisoner called -
JULIA CALNAN. I live at No. 2, George-street, Foley-place, Regent's-park. Last Saturday week I was at the prisoner's father's apartment from half-past one o'clock till half-past four - his father is my brother; I went there in consequence of a letter I had from Ireland - the prisoner was there; I believe his brother knew Mr. Bell; the prisoner was not sitting with his mother and me - his brother is older than him.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-272]
750. JAMES NEEDS was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of February, 3 bushels of potatoes, value 8s., and 1 sack, value 1s., the goods of William Hobbs.
WILLIAM HOBBS. I am a farmer, and live at Enfield. The prisoner was my carter; on the 19th of February I sent him, and some more men, to drive some potatoes home from the field: I ordered the men to load ten sacks in each cart, and no more - I went home and told my sister to mind what came in - I then went to market.
GEORGE ROBINSON. I am in Hobb's employ; he ordered me to put ten sacks into each cart; the prisoner drove one cart: I saw that every cart was loaded with ten sacks- none of them had more nor less, I am certain; he had to take them to the yard, which is three-quarters of a mile from the field.
REBECCA HOBBS. I am the prosecutor's sister: he told me to watch every cart; the prisoner came into the yard several times - I went out each time to see him unload, and one time he brought nine full sacks and one empty - I said nothing to him, but told my brother when he came home.
THOMAS COREHAM. I am in Mr. Hobb's employ; he told me to assist in unloading the potatoes; the prisoner came with one load of nine sacks full and one empty - all the other loads were ten full sacks; I said nothing to him about it.
JOHN WILSON. I am an officer, and took the prisoner; he said there was not a sack lost - that he shot a few potatoes over a gate, but who had them he did not know.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-273]
751. JOHN PHILLIPS was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of February, 1 pair of shoes, value 3s., the goods of Thomas Blizard.
JAMES GIBBS. I am a butcher, and live at Hollywell-lane. On the 16th of February, about half-past five o'clock, I saw a boy go and take a pair of shoes from Mr. Blizard's shop; I ran out and pursued the prisoner and another - I caught the prisoner in Shoreditch, and saw the shoes close by him; I cannot swear he took them.
THOMAS BLIZARD. These are my shoes - I know nothing of the prisoner.
SAMUEL STUDD. I heard a cry of Stop thief! ran out, and saw the prisoner drop these shoes - I am sure he is the boy.
GUILTY. Aged 12.
Whipped and Discharged.

752. DAVID DAVIS was indicted for feloniously receiving 1 watch, value 1l.; 1 seal, value 5s.; 1 key, value 3s., and 1 ring, value 2s., the goods of Michael Dibley, knowing the same to be stolen.
The evidence was the same in this case as that produced on the trial of Morris and others, page 321, except that the witness, Kett, deposed to the prisoner being in the house at the time of the robbery, and sitting on the same bench with Morris, who was seen to give him the watch.
NOT GUILTY.

753. JOHN MAYO was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of December, 2 sheets, value 7s., and 1 pillow, value 5s., the goods of George Jones.
ANN JONES. I am the wife of George Jones, a shoemaker. I let the prisoner a bed in November last - I lost the sheets and pillow, and have not found them; nobody else lodged in the house except a very respectable school-master - the prisoner went out early in the morning on the 1st of December: and I did not see him again till the other day when I met him at Bayswater, and he offered to pay me for them at so much a week - I gave charge of him.
Prisoner. I said I would give anything to her, as I was going to a situation.
GUILTY. Aged 49.

654. SARAH LEWIN was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of January, 1 pair of shoes, value 5s., and 1 tablecloth, value 5s., the goods of Ambrose Bradley.
AMBROSE BRADLEY. I am a pawnbroker. The prisoner occasionally charred at my house, and was there from the 12th to the 26th of January, washing a quantity of goods - she absconded. I had her apprehended, and duplicates of the articles were found on her.
EDWARD RUBERY. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Osborne-street, Whitechapel. I have a table-cloth, and a pair of shoes, pawned by Green, in her own name.
ANN GREEN. I pawned these things for the prisoner; I believed them to be her own.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JOSHUA CLEMENTS. I am an officer. I found the duplicates of these, and other things on the prisoner; she said she meant to return them.
