755. MICHAEL MORAN was indicted for stealing, on the 25th February, 1 opera-glass, value 2l., the goods of James Ils Chaffin, from his person.
JAMES ILES CHAFFIN. On the 25th of February I was returning with a friend from Drury-lane Theatre - I was under the Piazza in Covent-garden, near James-street, and had an opera-glass in my right-hand coat pocket (I do not reside in town) - I felt my pocket lighter; an officer came and told me I had been robbed, and produced it. I had not seen the prisoner.
JOHN BOSTON. I am a watchman, in the employ of the Commissioners of Woods and Forests. I was returning home, and saw the prisoner and another go up to this gentleman; the prisoner took the glass out of his pocket - I seized them both, but was obliged to let the other go, as there were a number of boys and girls round; I took the glass from him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. He has a spite against me.
GUILTY. Aged 11.
Transported for Seven Years.
The prisoner was convicted of felony at the Westminster Sessions, in July, and sentenced to two months' imprisonment and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18280221-278]
756. THOMAS KERSHAW was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of February, 1 half-crown, and 1 shilling, the monies of John Jones.
CLARA GREER. I am servant to John Jones, a tobacconist. I saw the prisoner at our shop three weeks ago, and served him with some tobacco, which came to seven farthings - he gave me a sovereign; I went into the parlour for change - my mistress came into the shop, and gave it to him: he then said he wanted 10s. worth of halfpence - she said she had not got it; he gave the silver back, and said it was of no use without, and paid for the tobacco in halfpence; my mistress put the silver on the counter, and when he was gone we missed 3s. On the 23d he came again for half an ounce of tobacco, and again gave me a sovereign; I went in and told my mistress I thought he was the man who took the 3s.; she then gave me six half-crowns and five shillings, which I took to him; he said,"It is of no use unless you can give me 10s. in halfpence- I told you so," but he had not; he put the silver back into my hand, and was putting his hand into his waistcoat pocket, for money to pay for his tobacco; I looked at the silver, and missed a half-crown; I said, "You have got half a crown" - he said he had not; my master came out of the room, and laid hold of him - he dropped the half-crown, and a shilling which I had not missed.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Had you returned his sovereign? A. It laid at the end of the counter: he had not taken it up when I accused him of it; he had not attempted to take it up.
JOHN JONES. I keep this shop. The prisoner came and the girl came in, and said he was the man who had been before; I saw six half-crowns and five shillings given to her, which she went and gave him; he said, "I want 10s. in halfpence;" she said we had none - he put back the silver; she accused him of keeping the half-crown: I came out, and took him; he dropped the half-crown and shilling - I detained him.
Cross-examined. Q. Were you near enough to hear what he said? A. Yes - I believe the sovereign was on the counter, but cannot say.
WILLIAM MORGAN. I was sent for, and found on the prisoner a sovereign, two shillings, and three pence.
Prisoner's Defence. I asked for 10s. worth of copper.
GUILTY. Aged 54.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-279]
757. JOHN HERRING was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of February, 1 shawl, value 10s.; 4 dead ducks, value 3s.; 1 bonnet, value 10s., 1 half-sovereign, and 23 shillings, the property of James Steward.
ELLEN STEWARD. I am the wife of James Steward - we live in a small stable, in Stratton-mews, Bloomsbury. On Friday night I was at home - my husband was in the country; a little girl slept with me, and her brother in the same room; I went to bed about eleven o'clock - all was fastened then. I awoke, and saw the window open - it is one story high; there are carts in the yard: I think a person could get up without a ladder. The girl awoke me, saying the window was open, and there was a thief in the room; I looked up, and saw the prisoner in his shirt-sleeves, and his black waistcoat - he blew out the rushlight, took off the counterpane, and covered his face, and ran down stairs - we called Murder! and Thieves! I got up, and went to his place, where his wife lays in; he lives next door to us - we knew him well; he was not there: the shawl and ducks were found behind a cart in the mews, with his jacket.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you speak to the man? A. No, I was afraid. I never accused any body else of it; I know Flood - he slept with the prisoner- I did not accuse him of it, but said he must have puthim up to get into the room. My property was all safe when I went to bed.
JAMES RUTH. I am thirteen years old, and slept in the room; I was awake, and saw the prisoner come to the drawers - I am certain of him; I knew him before: I called out; Mrs. Steward was awake - he put out the rushlight, which stood on the table, close to the bed. I saw him take the money from the drawers.
Cross-examined. Q. Were you not rather frightened? A. Yes. I did not tell the Magistrate he was the man, but I am sure he is.
GEORGE PRATT. I am a watchman. At a quarter to two o'clock in the morning, I heard a rattle spring, and ran to the spot; I found this shawl, two pairs of wild fowls, and the prisoner's jacket behind a cart in the mews.
ELLEN STEWARD. I know this to be the prisoner's jacket - I am certain of it; I saw him with it on when he got his beer for supper.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-280]
758. WILLIAM HALL was indicted for stealing, on the 22d of February, 1 yard of toilinett, value 3s., the goods of James Hollywell.
WILLIAM EDWARDS. I am a constable, and live in Eight Feathers-court - the prosecutor's shop is in Holywell-street. On the 22d of February, at eight o'clock at night, I saw the prisoner and another person there; I passed them, and heard them say, "It is all right, go it;" I watched: they waited some time, and crossed - I saw the prisoner go in at the door, and take this piece of toilinett; I crossed over, and seized him with it - he struck me a violent blow; my hat and his own fell off - he ran; I pursued him into Angel-inn-yard, and lost sight of him there, as he got under the coaches; I had his hat - he asked me for it when he was taken, and I gave it to him.
JAMES HOLLYWELL. I keep this shop - this toilinett is mine.
JAMES STACK. I was standing at my shop door, and saw Edwards seize the prisoner; I am certain he is the man.
The prisoner pleaded intoxication, and ignorance of the offence.
WILLIAM EDWARDS. He was sober; he could run as fast as me.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-281]
759. ANN EAST was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of February, 1 coat, value 20s., and 6 yards of printed cotton, value 5s. the goods of Francis Tracey.
MARTHA TRACEY. I am the wife of Francis Tracey; we live in King's-road, Chelsea. I take in washing - the prisoner lodged with us, with a man who took the room. I missed this property on the 14th of February, between ten and eleven o'clock; there was nobody in the house but her - my husband told them to leave next day, and they did so - I have not found my things; I was in my kitchen very ill, and saw the prisoner go out with something brown in her apron, between ten and eleven o'clock; I thought it was like my husband's coat - I found that the key of her room opened my parlour - nobody else was in the house but an independant old gentleman.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. She left because you gave her warning? A. Yes. I missed the coat at seven o'clock in the evening - I did not suspect it was gone, as I had the key - it was half under her apron; my daughter was in the kitchen with me, and said it looked like her father's coat - my husband missed it at one o'clock, but said nothing to me about it - I cannot say how many people came to the house that day - I told the prisoner of it at night; she said she had not been out all day.
MARTHA TRACEY. I am ten years old. I saw the prisoner go out on Thursday with a bundle under her apron - I saw something like my father's coat - our parlour door is kept locked, and the street door is kept shut.
Cross-examined. Q. Where were you? A. In the kitchen with my mother; I did not tell her of it, for she saw it herself. My father came home between two and three o'clock - he went to lay down, and the cotton and coat were missed.
COURT. Q. Did you then say you thought you saw her take it out? A. No.
Prisoner's Defence. I was not out all day.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-282]
760. WILLIAM CHELTON was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of December, 7 cloaks, value 14l. the goods of William Waldegrave Packer.
WILLIAM WALDEGRAVE PACKER. I am an undertaker, and live in Whitechapel-road. On the 12th of December, I had a funeral of Mr. James Bates.
HANNAH PACKER. I am the prosecutor's wife. This funeral was performed on the 12th of December, about four o'clock in the afternoon - the corpse laid in our shop, and was to be buried about a quarter of a mile from our house; the prisoner came the day after the corpse came - he merely looked at it - appeared to shed tears over it, and he agreed with a man named Bone, who came with him, as to the orders he had given for the interment - Bone has not been seen since; Bone ordered ten cloaks in the prisoner's presence - he said thirty persons would come, but only ten would follow as mourners, and my husband was to prepare the cloaks; the prisoner came again next day, but only stood on the step, while the other man talked to me a little. On the day of the funeral, it had gone on with only two mourners - the prisoner and Bone came soon after, and said, "We have met your husband and the corpse, on the other side of the way, and he desires you will tie two cloaks on our shoulders; but we won't wait for hatbands; and he will wait in the street for us." I did so, believing my husband had sent them - they went away with the two cloaks - the prisoner returned in about three minutes without a cloak, and said their overlooker from the docks, was coming up the road from the docks, with some more of the men, and as 7l. had been raised for the funeral, they did not like them to appear without cloaks; I was to give him what cloaks I had got on my arm, and my husband would wait for them - I gave him all I had on my arm - I thought there were six, but he says there was but five; I have only seen one since - I am sure he said he came by my husband's authority.
ROBERT HENRY BURGESS. I am an officer. I received information on the 14th of January - I went to the Bull's head public-house, and took the prisoner there; heacknowledged to me next morning, that he had had the cloaks, but did not know what became of them, for he got tipsy, and lost them.
WILLIAM WALDEGRAVE PACKER. On the 12th of December I went on with the corpse; I met Bone and another man, who, I think, was the prisoner, with shovels in their hands; I went up to a mourner named Dawes, and said, "There are two of your men" - he said, "We cannot wait for them" - we went on and interred the corpse; when I returned, my wife enquired for the cloaks - I had sent nobody whatever to her for cloaks, or any thing - the whole story was false: the prisoner said at the office he did not know what had become of them, for some girls of the town put them on; one cloak has been returned - that was taken off Bone's bed, and brought to me by Dawes.
Prisoner's Defence. I never was there with Bone - I went with one Scroggins to see the corpse; I called at a public-house, and left about a dozen shovels there - I had met the men following the corpse; they said I might come if I liked - I said No, I would go to the house we were to meet at afterwards; I went next morning to Dawes' house - he said Bone had been to the funeral, and had not returned the cloaks.
GUILTY. Aged 32.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-283]
Fourth Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Common Sergeant.
761. JAMES GARRARD and ANDREW KEATING were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of January, 26lbs. weight of Sugar, value 14s., the goods of William Sewell and Jonathan Wholey.
EDWARD BARKER. I am warehousemen to William Sewell and Jonathan Wholey, wholesale grocers, of Upper Thames-street. I forwarded a hogshead of sugar to Mr. Beal, of Somers-town, on the 11th of January: it was brought from the Docks to our warehouse by Garrard, who is in the employ of a carman named Kay: I directed him to take it on to Beal's - this was about three o'clock in the afternoon; I examined the marks on it, and took the weight from the dock note - I knew nothing of Keating.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You did not examine it? A. I took the marks of it down as it was in the cart.
WILLIAM BOOTH. I am a chemist. Between five and six o'clock on the 11th of January; I was going along Battle Bridge-road - it snowed; I met the prisoners there- they could not get on with the waggon; I and some others assisted them - Keating got into the waggon between two hogsheads, which were in it: they got as far as Weston-street, and there they staid some time; we found fault with Garrard for using his horses so badly, - I then walked round the waggon, and Keating jumped out - thinking something was wrong, I pursued and took him; he said, "Pray let me go," and asked for his hat.
Prisoner KEATING. Q. Did I not ask you where Garrard was? A. No.
MILLIAM LUMLEY. I live in Cottage-place, City-road. I saw the waggon - Booth was talking to Garrard about whipping his horses so much; I caught hold of the horses head, and then Booth went back, and was talking under a light to a man who I could not see; Garrard then came up to me, used very bad language, and said, "What, did you never see a waggon with two horses before:" I then thought I saw something move in the waggon under the matting - I touched it with my umbrella, and Keating jumped out; we caught him, and he was taken to the watch-house: Beal came up and said, "This is my sugar, and was coming to my house" - the hogsheads were then taken to his house, and it was found 26 lbs. had been taken out of one - a few nails were found on Keating; he said he lived in the Commercial-road - there was a bag of sugar - I saw it at Mr. Beal's; I think I saw a man bring it from the waggon, but I cannot swear that - it was opened, and contained sugar, which Beale compared with the hogshead.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was it dark? A. Not very - there were gas-lights; I do not think it snowed at that time; I think I did see the bag taken from the cart - I may have sworn so at the office.
Prisoner KEATING. Q. Did I not tell you Garrard had told me to get up and fill the bag? A. He said as we went to the watch-house, that the carman asked him to come and get a little sugar.
THOMAS BEAL. I am a grocer, and live in Skinner-street, St. Pancras. I expected two hogsheads of sugar on the 11th of January, from the prosecutors - one came in the morning; about seven o'clock I had information - I went to Brewer-street, and saw Booth bringing Keating back; I went up to the waggon, and between two hogsheads of sugar, I found a bag of sugar, and some was coming out of the hogshead into the bag then; the tin plate which covered the sample-hole, was knocked off, and twisted round - it hung by one nail; the waggon was brought to my house, and the hogshead and the bag brought in; the sugar in the bag was the same as that in the hogshead; I weighed the hogshead, and it was 26lbs. deficient.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. What is over the sample-hole? A. A piece of tin nailed on; it could not have got rubbed off - a chisel or a knife will get it off.
RICHARD COOPER. I am a constable, and took Keating in charge. I found a few nails on him - they are not the same sort as the one left in the tin.
ROBERT TEASDALE. I am an officer. I produce a bag of sugar, which I received from Mr. Beal; I saw the hogshead weighed - I was before the Magistrate - what the prisoners said was taken down, and read over to them; Garrard said he met Keating in Gray's Inn-lane.
THOMAS BEAL. The nails found on Keating would nail the tin on - it is done with any kind of nails; there was a chasm in the sample-hole; I have no doubt the sugar in the bag was the same as that in the hogshead - part of it was running out when I took it.
MR. CHURCHILL. Q. If the plate had come off by accident, could they do better than put the bag to catch it? A. No; but that quantity could not have come out of itself; the proper way would be to turn the hole to the top.
KEATING'S Defence. I met Garrard in Gray's Inn-lane- he could not get on: the sugar was running out - he told me to get up and get a little out, in a bag; I picked up the nails on Galley-quay.
GARRARD'S Defence. I met him, and said I would give him a pint of beer to go with me; the tin had robbed off, by rubbing against the other hogshead - I had a bag, and told him to get in and catch it; the tin was nearly off when I had it.
JOHN HALL. I am a publican. I have known Garrard all his life; I have worked in the docks as an operative cooper, and have frequently seen sugar wasting in the Commercial-road; from the friction of two hogsheads together; the tin, being nailed in the bulge, might very possibly rub off; I have known the tin actually worn through.
Garrard received a good character.
GARRARD - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Six Months.
KEATING - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-284]
762. GEORGE CHAMBERS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 5 pieces of deal boards, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Louis England.
MR. BARRY conducted the prosecution.
LOUIS ENGLAND. I am a timber-merchant - the prisoner worked for me as a carpenter. On the 20th of February I lost five pieces of wood; I found them at the watch-house next day, and compared them with the boards they were cut from - they corresponded; I went to his lodging, and found some more wood, worth above 2l.
Cross-examined by MR. BODKIN. Q. I believe you did not suspect him before? A. No; I do not allow my men any wood; they have been cut to make a washing-tray - I know it by several marks on it - it has my foreman's name on it.
JAMES FOX. I am watchman of the Back-road, Islington. On the 20th of February, about six o'clock in the evening; I stopped the prisoner, and found this wood in his basket.
Cross-examined. Q. Did he not tell you where he had got wood? A. Yes, and said he brought it from the City road basin.
THOMAS GRAFTON. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house, and said he bought the wood at a timber-yard close to the City-road basin; I went there, and found he had not bought it there.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 46.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.

763. THOMAS CLARK was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of February, 1 lathe, value 50s., the goods of James Aston.
JAMES ASTON. I am a wood-turner, and live in St. Luke' s. On the morning of the 12th of February I missed my lathe; I found my back window broken; I had seen the lathe safe the day before.
HUMPHRY PERRY. I work for Aston, and was using the lathe till nine o'clock at night; I went at seven in the morning, found the window broken in, and the lathe gone.
WILLIAM ROBERTS. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Turnmill-street. On the 12th of February the prisoner brought me this lathe; I received information, and gave him in charge - he seemed anxious to go away; I am certain of his person; he escaped from the officer; he gave the name of Clark, and said he brought it from his master.
THOMAS HARRISON. I am a constable. I apprehended the prisoner, at his mother's, on the 20th, he said he heard I had been looking after him.
Prisoner's Defence. I told the officer I had it from the prosecutor's son; whom I met in Red Lion-street, and he asked me to go and pawn it, and say it was my master's; he stood on the other side of the way, and beckoned me, when I was detained - I went over to him.
THOMAS HARRISON. He gave this account on the 21st, the day he was committed; and the prosecutor's son said he had not seen the prisoner for six weeks; I knew the prisoner before; I went two or three times before I could find him; when I at first took him, he said he did not know the prosecutor's son.
JAMES ASTON. My son had left me, and I told the officer to take him, as I had some suspicion of him.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Six Months.

764. EDWARD BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of February, 3 shirts, value 1s.; 2 jackets, value 2s. 6d.; 1 pair of stockings, value 3d.; 3 pairs of trousers, value 1s.; 1 comforter, value 3d.; 1 handkerchief, value 6d., and 2 pairs of drawers, value 1s., the goods of Alexander Atkinson.
JOHN GIBSON. I keep a lodging-house, at Wapping. Atkinson lodged with me, and is gone to sea. The prisoner brought me a note, as from him, requesting I would deliver these clothes to him; I gave these articles to him; the prosecutor came for them himself, two days after; I said I had given them to the prisoner.
ELIZABETH KERSHAW. I took the prisoner, and found some of the property on him; he said it was done, and could not be helped, he might as well die as be starved.
Prisoner's Defence. I went on board a ship, and Atkinson gave me the note - he said he meant to give himself up as a deserter from the Tribune frigate.
ELIZABETH KERSHAW. He stated this before the Magistrate, and Atkinson denied it.
NOT GUILTY.

765. HANNAH BLAKE was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of February, 1 shirt, value 1s. 6d.; 2 pairs of stockings, value 1s. 6d.; 1 cravat, value 9d., and 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of William Staines.
WILLIAM STAINES. On the 21st of February, about eight o'clock in the morning, I was at the Nag's Head public-house, Hart-street, Covent-garden. I called for a pipe - I had this property in a bundle; the prisoner came and sat opposite to me; she came in and out two or three times, with a man named Roberts; they had a pint of beer and a quartern of gin; she told the people in the tap-room, she had just come out of the House of Correction - I missed her and my bundle; I said to the man who came with her,"My bundle is gone;" he drank the beer and gin, and said he would go with me round the market, and see for her - we went, and could not find her; we returned to the house- I asked the man for his and her name and address, and sat down to write them, but the servant-girl brought this prisoner in, and said she met her coming out of a gin-shop- the prisoner said she had sold my things.
JOSEPH METCALF. I am an officer. I was fetched to the house about half-past ten o'clock in the morning; Staines produced this handkerchief, which he said had contained the property, and that the prisoner had given it to him.
Prisoner's Defence. I had just come out of confinement, and had no place to go to; through my husband forsaking me.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.

766. JOHN HICKMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 1 coat, value 10s., the goods of James Goodale; and that he, (the prisoner,) before the commission of the said felony, (to wit,) on the 4th day of December, in the fourth year of his Majesty's reign, by the name of John Asbury, was convicted of felony.
JAMES GOODALE. On the night of the 4th of February, about nine o'clock, I left my horse and gig in the care of a young man, while I went to a house opposite Compton-street - in about two minutes I heard a rattle - I ran out, and saw some person with my coat, which I had left in the gig.
JAMES PROTHEROE. I saw the gig at the door of the King's Arms; I saw the prisoner go and take the coat - I followed and took him - I saw him drop it.
WILLIAM BALLARD. I am an officer. I produce a certificate of the prisoner's former conviction - it is signed by Mr. Shelton, I know the prisoner to be the person named therein.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not at first identify me as John Groves? A. I mistook the name; Groves was connected with him - I knew him the moment I saw him, and said"Your name is Groves;" but on referring to my book, I found his name was Asbury.
The certificate of the prisoner's former conviction, was here put in and read.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Life.

767. THOMAS LAND was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of February, 2 sheets, value 20s., the goods of William Snowley; and 1 Prayer-book, value 1s., and 1 handkerchief, value 4d., the goods of Mary Quick.
FANNY SNOWLEY. I am the wife of William Snowley; he keeps the Bell and Mackarel, in Mile End-road . The prisoner had lodged there three nights - these sheets did not belong to his bed.
MARY QUICK. I am servant at this house. I lost my my Prayer-book and handkerchief from my box which was in my bed-room.
WILLIAM WILSON. I am an officer. About ten o'clock in the morning of the 25th of February, I saw the prisoner go into a shop at Shadwell, and come out with a bundle; I followed and took him - I asked what he had got; he said two sheets, which he had brought from Dover; that he brought them from Jamaica; they were tied in this handkerchief, and the Prayer-book was in his hat.
JOSEPH GALLOWAY. I am an officer, and took the prisoner to Greenwich where he said he had lodged. I found it was false; he then said I had had a deal of trouble, and he would tell me - that he took them from the Bell and Mackarel.
Prisoner's Defence. I have been twenty-two years in his Majesty's service, and was in distress.

769. WILLIAM OLIFF was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, 1 sack, value 1s. 6d., and three pecks of beans and oats, value 1s. 6d., the goods of William Adamson.
WILLIAM ADAMSON. I am a market-gardener. The prisoner was in my employ, and had the care of my corn; this sack has my initials on it, and is mine - he has a wife and three children.
THOMAS SKITTERELL. I am a watchman to Mr. Adamson. On Sunday evening I saw this sack in the stable; prisoner was there, and said, "I am going to Stamford-hill; I shall go out the back way; don't take any notice of it." I saw him go out towards Clapton; I followed him, and found him with this sack and corn, which I believe to be master's.
ROBERT BROWN. I took the prisoner, with the sack and corn; he said it was the first time.
Prisoner. I am very sorry for it.
The prisoner received a good character.

Nichols for 6s.; but as there was a ticket marked 16s. 6d. on it, I detained her.
GEORGE WHIPPLE. I am apprentice to Sarah Nichols and Simon Latter, pawnbrokers, Ratcliffe; this shawl is theirs, and was safe between five and six o'clock that evening; I did not miss it till Mr. Harris came.

772. HENRY DENHAM was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of February, 2 curtains, value 5s.; 1 counterpane, value 5s.; 2 blankets, value 8s.; 2 sheets, value 6s., and 2 pillows, value 6s., the goods of Ann Atlee, widow.
ANN ATLEE. I live in Turnmill-street. The prisonerlodged with me for nine days; on the 2d of February he came and gave my child a book, and said he should work at home next week - I said the child could not read, and told him I wanted a flat iron: he fetched me down two from his room; he and his wife left that night - they took the key, and never returned; I broke the door open on Monday, and missed all this property, none of which has been found.
JOHN BALLARD. I am a constable. I took the prisoner at the Horns, Kennington; I told him the charge - he said"I surrender myself to you, in order to exonerate the girl," alluding to a girl I had taken before - I found some money on him.
Prisoner's Defence. I can only deny it - the young woman I lived with took the room; the things were all safe when I left - I said I would exonerate the girl, because she left two hours before me, but I did not take the things; the door is always open, and there are several lodgers - I locked the door, and left the key in it when I left; I cannot recover my character, and if I am transported it will be a pleasure to me - but declare I am innocent.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-295]
773. GEORGE RIGGS was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of February, 2 shillings, the monies of Thomas Groves.
MR. CHURCHILL conducted the prosecution.
HENRY DEWDNEY. I am clerk to Thomas Groves. The prisoner was in his service; on the 18th of February I gave him 10s., with a written order to get some nails - I told him to bring a bill of them; he returned with this bill, which amounts to 8s. 8d. - he brought me 1s. 4d; I told him one item was much overcharged; and on Wednesday morning, as he was going by the shop, I desired him to call and get it corrected - Mr. Adams sent the bill back.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Where had you sent him? A. To Messrs. Adams and Morris; I think I mentioned the mistake to him on the 18th - he staid with us till Thursday morning, when he was taken.
WILLIAM SALKELD ADAMS. I am shopman to Morris and Adams. On the 18th of February the prisoner came with an order; I sent the goods with this bill - it amounted to 6s. 8d., but since it left our shop a figure of 2s. has been added to an item of 11d., and the 6s. at the bottom is altered to 8s.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you enter it in your book? A. Yes, it is 6s. 8d. there - I looked at the entry yesterday; I am certain of it without referring to the book.
COURT. Q. Is the bill now in the state the prisoner brought it to you? A. It is.
MR. PHILLIPS. Q. When he returned did he not tell you he had made a mistake? A. He gave the bill to Mr. Adams, and said it was a mistake; Mr. Adams called me and said it was a mistake of mine - I said it was not; the prisoner wished me to write a note, and give him the bill back, but I would not and kept it, saying there was a gross imposition some where.
Q. Was not your deposition copied three time? A. The clerk made so many mistakes, the Magistrate said he would have them copied again, or they could not be read.
COURT. Q. Are you quite sure the alterations in the bill are not your making? A. I am.
The prisoner put in a written defence, declaring his innocence.
HENRY SPICER. I took the prisoner in charge - I remember the mistakes being made in Adam's deposition; it was taken by a new clerk - the Magistrate had them copied again.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 22.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18280221-296]

775. JAMES CARROLL was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of January, 1 boot, value 4s., the goods of Lucy Pendrill.
LUCY PENDRILL. I am a widow, and live in Angel-street, St. Martin's-le-grand. On the 31st of January my two boys were playing in my shop - one of them called Stop thief! and the prisoner was brought back with this boot.
GEORGE PENDRILL. I was in my mother's shop, and saw the prisoner come in and take a boot off the shelf - I called Stop thief! he dropped it, and ran away; I am sure he is the man.
HEZEKIAH WILMER. About seven o'clock on the 31st of January, I saw the prisoner at the corner of this window, and soon after heard a cry of Stop thief! I ran back, and took the prisoner, who was running from the shop; the boy said he had taken the boot.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 16.

776. BENJAMIN NATHAN and THOMAS FELL were indicted for stealing, on the 18th of February, 24 1/2lbs. of cheese, value 15s., the goods of Isaac Shaw.
ROBERT WILLAVIZE. I am shopman to Mr. Isaac Shaw, a cheesemonger, of Gracechurch-street. I did not miss this cheese till the officer brought it in on the 21st of February - I knew it to be Mr. Shaw's, by a cross on it, and a piece of tin was nailed on it; I doubt whether it had been sold; a lady was looking at it on the Saturday - she objected to it on account of its being bored so many times.
Cross-examined by MR. J. ALLEY. Q. Do you know who made the cross on it? A. My father, in the country- it is a common cross; I swear to it by a tin tack on it, where the tin was; it stood at the door, and was marked 7 1/2d. - I know nothing of the prisoner.
CHARLES THOROGOOD. I am an officer. On the 18th I saw Nathan turn the corner of Lime-street, with something under his arm, which he gave to Fell, and walked back himself; I followed Fell, and saw him putting this cheese into a bag - I went up, and asked what he had there; he said a cheese, which he brought from Rose-lane, and then he said he was going to take it there; Nathan came up just afterwards, and I took them both into custody; I went to several shops, and at last to Mr. Shaw's, where they claimed it.
Cross-examined. Q. What time was this? A. About a quarter-past six o'clock.
ROBERT WILLAVIZE. I am sure the cheese was safe on Saturday, and am certain it is Mr. Shaw's.
NATHAN'S Defence. I was coming from Westminster, and the officer took me. I know nothing of the other prisoner.
Nathan received a good character.
NATHAN - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined Three Months.
FELL - NOT GUILTY.

777. SAMUEL GEORGE OWENS was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of January, 29 ozs. of bacon, value 1s. 9d.; 16 ozs. of butter, value 1s.; 5 eggs, value 6d.; 1 half-crown, 12 shillings, 4 sixpences, and 5 pence, the property of Thomas Raine; and ELIZABETH (HIS WIFE) was indicted for feloniously inciting, ordering, procuring, &c. the said Samuel George Owens, the said felony to do and commit.
THOMAS RAINE. I am a cheesemonger, and live in Houndsditch. On the 24th of January the prisoner Elizabeth came and ordered these articles to be sent to No. 18, Jewry-street, with change for a sovereign; I sent them - my boy brought back a bad sovereign, having left the articles, and the change.
JOSEPH DYER. I am servant to Mr. Raine. I went with the articles and change to Jewry-street; just before I got to the house the male prisoner met me, and said,"Are you going to No. 18, Jewry-street?" I said Yes; he blowed me up for being so long; he said, "My master has sent me for the things - give them to me; have you brought change for a sovereign?" I said Yes - he gave me a sovereign, and said, "Go back and fetch 1lb. of Cheshire cheese;" I took back the same sovereign to master - he sounded it, and it was bad; I then went to No. 18, Jewry-street, and they knew nothing about it.
WILLIAM RILEY. I am an officer. I received information, and took the male prisoner on the 2d, and on the 4th the woman came to see him - I detained her.
S. G. OWENS - GUILTY. Aged 25.
Transported for Seven Years.

778. ANN BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of January, 5 pinafores, value 1s.; 1 petticoat, value 6d.; 2 pillow-cases, value 3s.; 2 bed-gowns, value 1s.; 3 shifts, value 2s.; 5 pairs of stockings, value 1s.; 2 frocks, value 1s.; 2 pairs of gloves, value 6d., and 1 handkerchief, value 4d., the goods of John Osborne.
MISS ANN PINDER OSBORNE. I am the daughter of John Osborne, of No. 4, St. Dunstan's-passage, Tower-street. The prisoner was in our service - she was leaving on the 27th of January, and we missed this property; I sent for an officer, who searched her, and found the duplicates on her; he went and got the articles - one petticoat was found on her person.
MARY READ. I am a dress-maker. On the Friday before, the prisoner brought this cotton to our house, and said she had got some stuff to make a gown; she left it with me, called again, and said she had not time to tell me how to make it, but would call again; the officer afterwards called, and I gave it to him.
JOHN THOMPSON. I am an officer. I was fetched to Mr. Osborne's, and took the prisoner; I found the property by the duplicates.
Prisoner's Defence. I am a most unfortunate woman since my husband has left me. I throw myself on your mercy.
GUILTY. Aged 39.
Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-301]
799. THOMAS MILLER was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of February, 2 bottles, value 4d., and 3 pints of wine, value 5s., the goods of Henry Mann and another.
HENRY MANN. I am in partnership with Alexander Maloney. The prisoner was in my brother's service, and often brought bottles to our premises.
ROBERT BENCE. I am cellarman to the prosecutors. On the 11th of January, about five o'clock in the evening, the prisoner brought some bottles to their cellar in Mincing-lane; I heard bottles rattle, looked round, and saw him putting one into his pocket; I asked what he was doing - he said he had picked it up in the street; I desired him to produce it, which he did; I then told him to unbutton himself, and saw one bottle sticking out of his breeches pocket; there was wine in each of them - I gave him in charge.
JAMES HALL. I took the prisoner in charge.
The prisoner pleaded intoxication.
GUILTY. Aged 36.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18280221-302]
780. JOHN JONES and THOMAS SMITH were indicted for stealing, on the 4th of February, 6 neckerchiefs, value 35s., the goods of John Freeman.
JOHN FREEMAN. I live in Bishopsgate-street. On the 4th of February I lost these handkerchiefs - I was not at home at the time; I know they were in my shop on the 4th- they have my mark on them.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Had you sold any of them? A. Not any.
CHARLES STANTON. I am servant to Mr. Freeman. On the 4th of February I saw the prisoners in the shop for a quarter of an hour, but neither saw them come in nor go out; Forrester brought them into the shop in about five minutes, with the handkerchiefs - some ladies were being served at the time.
DANIEL FORRESTER. On the 4th of February, about half-past four o'clock, I saw the two prisoners go into Mr. Freeman's shop; another lad stood outside; I waited tillthey came out, then followed, and took them at the Marine Society gateway - I took off Smith's hat, and found three of the handkerchiefs; the other three were behind Jones, in the mud - he had dropped them.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Did you see them fall? A. No; they were close behind him; Smith was about a yard off - they could not have fallen from him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
JONES' Defence. I went to buy a black handkerchief; they wanted 4s., and I did not buy it - I had not seen Smith; nothing was found on me.
SMITH'S Defence. I was not in the shop.
JONES - GUILTY. Aged 14.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.

JOHN SURRIDGE. I am a chesse-monger, and live in Fenchurch-street. On the 4th of February the officer called, and asked if I had lost any cheese; I missed one from the door.
CHARLES THOROGOOD. On the 4th of February I was in the street, and saw the prisoner and another; I watched them, and saw the prisoner go and take this cheese from Mr. Surridge's door; he crossed over, and I stopped him with it - he said he had 9d. given him to carry it from London-bridge to Mile-end-road; he had called to the other to take it, but he would not - the other got away.

JOB VALENTINE WATKINS. I am shopman to Jacob Russell, a pawnbroker, of Fore-street. On the 26th of January I heard a noise at our door, and saw two handkerchiefs drawn down; I ran, and saw the prisoner with them- I followed, and, just as I got to him, he dropped them; I secured him.

783. WILLIAM BLACKMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of February, 1 truck, value 4l., the goods of George Durant and another.
JAMES ROBERTS. I am a constable. On the 14th of February, about a quarter-past seven o'clock at night, I saw the prisoner on Tower-hill, with two others; they conversed about five minutes, and then separated; the prisoner and another went into a dark corner, and took a truck - they took it up the Minories; I went and asked the other man if he knew what they were going to do with it; he said No; I then followed the prisoner and his companion - they took it on to the corner of Goodman's-yard, where I stopped them, and asked what they were going to do with it; they both ran off - I pursued, and took the prisoner, without losing sight of him; Ellis claimed the truck.
MORRIS ELLIS. I live with Mr. George Durant and his partner, of Winchester-street; Roberts showed me the truck - I had missed it about half-past six o'clock in the evening of the 14th, from the door; I know nothing of the prisoner.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18280221-306]
784. WILLIAM PAGE was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of January, 1 hat-band, value 4s., the goods of Henry Allen.
HENRY ALLEN. I am an undertaker. On the 24th of January I attended a funeral at Aldgate church - the prisoner was there as an undertaker's man; we went to the Turk's Head public-house, Whitechapel, and I missed my hat-band off the back parlour table; the prisoner was searched, and it was found between his waistcoat and shirt- I had known him about six weeks.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you not all beat him? A. He was knocked down, and I struck him; he joined with the others in saying, "Let us all be searched again:" they were searched once, and it was not found - I believe he bears a very respectable character.
WILLIAM MARKWELL. I am an officer. I searched all the men, but found nothing; they then all desired to be again searched, and under the prisoner's waistcoat I found the band - he was struck.
Cross-examined. Q. Did not they all strike him? A. Yes; he requested to be searched; I have known him five years; he bears a very respectable character.
Prisoner's Defence. I am quite innocent; it was not my intention to steal it.
The prisoner received a most excellent character.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18280221-307]
785. CHARLES ROBINSON was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of February, 1 pair of bellows, value 2s. 9d., the goods of Uriah Bryant and another.
ADAM URIAH BRYANT. I am in partnership with Uriah Bryant, my father; we are furnishing ironmongers, and live in Cripplegate. On the 7th of February I saw the prisoner come four or five feet into the shop, and take these bellows off the nail; he just turned round, but whether he was going to take them, I do not know - he saw me, and put them on the nail again, and went out - he had matches to sell; I called him two or three times, and he returned - I cannot say he intended to steal them.
NOT GUILTY.
